========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, I'm here because... well, honestly, it feels like nobody is listening to me, and people keep acting like I'm just making things up or imagining it all. But I'm not. I know for a fact, I mean I've seen the signs, that the hospital people and even some folks from the housekeeping agency I work for have been watching our house. And not just the house, they've been following my cleaning routes, too. I clean a lot of houses over on the west side, you know, the big ones with the long driveways, and I've noticed the same cars parked down the street, or people just standing around when I'm taking the trash out. I know what they're doing. They're checking up on me to see if I'm taking proper care of my husband. He's been having a hard time lately, and I do everything for him, I really do, but it's like they don't believe I can handle it. They think because I'm out working all day, scrubbing floors and dusting baseboards, that I'm neglecting him. But I make his meals before I leave, I make sure his pills are laid out... and still, they're watching. I can feel them watching, trying to catch me making a mistake. It's just exhausting, you know? Always having to look over my shoulder while I'm just trying to do my job and take care of my family.

Harm me? Oh, heavens no, no, I don't think they want to hurt me physically or anything like that. And I'm certainly nobody special, just a regular woman trying to keep her head above water, you know? I'm not a politician or a celebrity, I'm just a housekeeper. I've been cleaning houses for, gosh, going on thirty years now. Started right out of high school when my aunt got me a job at the downtown hotel, and then I moved to residential because the hours were better for my family. So I'm just a normal person. It's not about me being important or having some big secret role. 

It's really just about my husband, and how they think I'm failing him. You see, he has had these terrible problems with his health, and he just sits in his recliner all day—that big brown leather one we got on sale a few years ago, it has a little tear in the arm now but he won't let me put a patch on it—and he really can't do much for himself right now. And people talk. Oh, you know how people talk, especially the nurses and the social workers from when he was in the hospital last time. They gave me these looks, these awful, judgmental looks when they were going over his discharge papers, like they thought I wasn't paying attention. 

They see me leaving at 6:00 AM with my supplies, and they think, "Look at her, leaving that poor sick man all alone all day while she goes off to work." But they don't see what goes on inside our home! They don't see that I've already made his oatmeal, and I've put his pills in those little plastic days-of-the-week boxes. The blue compartments for the morning, the red ones for the evening. I do everything right! I really do. But they don't believe it. So that's why they're watching. They want to catch me slipping up. They want to see if I forget to come home on time, or if I stop at the grocery store for too long, just to prove that I'm unfit to take care of him. It's not about hurting me, it's about catching me in a mistake so they can say they were right about me all along. They just think I'm not good enough, even though I work my fingers to the bone every single day for that man.

Well, no, it always comes back to him. It always comes back to my husband and the house, because that's my whole life, isn't it? I mean, like I said when I first sat down here, the whole reason I'm even talking to you is because people keep acting like I'm just... imagining all this. Like I'm making it up out of thin air! My sister even said to me on the phone last Tuesday—or maybe it was Wednesday, because I was dusting the Miller's living room and they always have me come on Wednesdays now—she said, "Oh, you're just stressed, you're just seeing things." 

But I'm not! I know what I see. I know for an absolute fact that the hospital people, the ones who discharged him last month, and even some of the supervisors from the housekeeping agency, they are watching our house. And they don't just stay at the house, either. They follow my cleaning routes. I've got five houses on the west side, like I told you, and I drive my little blue sedan from one to the next, and I see them. I see the same silver car parked down the block, or someone standing near the bus stop who doesn't ever get on a bus. They just wait, and they watch me hauling my vacuum out of the trunk, watching to see how long I take, checking to see if I'm rushing through my work so I can get home, or if I'm taking too long and leaving him alone. It's all about whether I'm taking proper care of him. They're just waiting for me to fail, waiting to point the finger and say I can't handle it. But I'm not crazy, and I'm not imagining it, no matter what anybody says. I see them with my own two eyes.

And people talk. Oh, you know how people talk, especially the nurses and the social workers from when he was in the hospital last time. They gave me these looks, these awful, judgmental looks when they were going over his discharge papers, like they thought I wasn't paying attention. \n\nThey see me leaving at 6:00 AM with my supplies, and they think, \"Look at her, leaving that poor sick man all alone all day while she goes off to work.\" But they don't see what goes on inside our home! I do everything right! I really do. But they don't believe it. So that's why they're watching. They want to catch me slipping up. They want to see if I forget to come home on time, or if I stop at the grocery store for too long, just to prove that I'm unfit to take care of him. It's not about hurting me, it's about catching me in a mistake so they can say they were right about me all along. They just think I'm not good enough, even though I work my fingers to the bone every single day for that man.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other situations where you feel people are acting against you or monitoring you, outside of the concern about caring for your husband?\n\nPatient: Well, no, it always comes back to him. I know what I see. I see the same silver car parked down the block, or someone standing near the bus stop who doesn't ever get on a bus. It's all about whether I'm taking proper care of him. They're just waiting for me to fail, waiting to point the finger and say I can't handle it. Well, doctor, like I was just trying to explain... I mean, it feels like we're going in circles a little bit, because this is exactly why I'm sitting here in this chair today. I'm here because people keep acting like I'm just imagining it all. They look at me like I'm making up stories, but I'm not! I know what I see. 

I don't have any other things going on, no other reasons for people to follow me. It is entirely about him. I know for a fact that the hospital people—the ones who were so judgmental when he was discharged—and some of the folks from the housekeeping agency I work for have been watching our house. And they don't just stay parked on our street, either. They have been watching my cleaning routes, following me when I drive from a deep clean over on Elm Street to a standard wipe-down on Maple. I see them keeping tabs on me while I'm lugging my mops and buckets around. 

And it is all just to see if I'm taking proper care of my husband. That's the only reason. There's no other big secret going on. They just don't believe a woman who cleans houses all day can also be a good wife to a sick man. They want to catch me slipping up, they want to see if I'm neglecting him, which I'm not! I do everything for him. But nobody listens to me when I tell them what's happening. They just act like it's all in my head, and it just wears me out having to keep explaining myself when I know exactly what they're doing.

proportions).\n  manifestation: I started feeling like I was the only one with the sense and strength to keep my husband, my home, and even some of the church people safe from what was coming.\n- key: S036\n  name: Decreased_Sleep\n  description: Decreased need for sleep (e.g., feels rested after only 3 hours of sleep).\n  manifestation: Many nights I sleep two or three hours and wake up ready to scrub floors or sort papers, not tired like I used to be after a short night.\n- key: S038\n  name: Racing_Thoughts\n  description: Flight of ideas or subjective experience that thoughts are racing.\n  manifestation: My mind jumps so fast from bills to Bible verses to my husband's breathing to which house I need to clean next that I can't catch one thought long enough to finish it.\n- key: S039\n  name: Manic_Distractibility\n  description: Attention is too easily drawn to unimportant or irrelevant external stimuli.\n  manifestation: If a car door shuts outside, the refrigerator hums, or someone moves a cup while I'm talking, my attention goes straight to that and I lose what I was doing.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: psychotic_symptoms and manic_episode occur concurrently\n  manifestation: During the weeks when I was sleeping very little and running from cleaning jobs to church errands, I was also hearing that woman's voice and feeling certain people were monitoring my routes.\n- key: additional_requirement_2\n  name: Additional Requirement 2\n  description: manic_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness\n  manifestation: For most of this whole change, I have been keyed up, irritable, and driven to keep moving, even in the stretches when things were not at their worst.\n- key: additional_requirement_3\n  name: Additional Requirement 3\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode\n  manifestation: There was a quieter spell for several weeks when I was sleeping more and not rushing around as much, but I still heard the voice by the kitchen window and believed the agency was watching us.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: My cleaning agency took away my supervisory routes because clients and coworkers said they could not rely on my work the way they used to. At home, my husband and pastor have had to step in more because I am not keeping up with caregiving and household responsibilities safely.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 year and 2 months\n  manifestation: This whole change has been going on for about 1 year and 2 months, since after my husband's cardiac hospitalization.\n- key: psychotic_symptoms_duration\n  name: Psychotic Symptoms Duration\n  description: 11 months\n  manifestation: The voices and the feeling that people are watching us have been happening for about 11 months now.\n- key: manic_episode_duration\n  name: Manic Episode Duration\n  description: 10 months\n  manifestation: The stretches of being unusually energized, irritable, and barely sleeping have been happening on and off for about 10 months.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 57 / maid_or_housekeeping_cleaner / high_school\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S019\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Do you believe people are watching or following you because they want to harm you, or do you feel like you have a special role or importance that others are trying to interfere with?\n\nPatient: Harm me? Oh, heavens no, no, I don't think they want to hurt me physically or anything like that. And I'm certainly nobody special, just a regular woman trying to keep her head above water, you know? I'm not a politician or a celebrity, I'm just a housekeeper. I've been cleaning houses for, gosh, going on thirty years now. Started right out of high school when my aunt got me a job at the downtown hotel, and then I moved to residential because the hours were better for my family. So I'm just a normal person. It's not about me being important or having some big secret role. \n\nIt's really just about my husband, and how they think I'm failing him. You see, he has had these terrible problems with his health, and he just sits in his recliner all day—that big brown leather one we got on sale a few years ago, it has a little tear in the arm now but he won't let me put a patch on it—and he really can't do much for himself right now. And people talk. Oh, you know how people talk, especially the nurses and the social workers from when he was in the hospital last time. They gave me these looks, these awful, judgmental looks when they were going over his discharge papers, like they thought I wasn't paying attention. \n\nThey see me leaving at 6:00 AM with my supplies, and they think, \"Look at her, leaving that poor sick man all alone all day while she goes off to work.\" But they don't see what goes on inside our home! They don't see that I've already made his oatmeal, and I've put his pills in those little plastic days-of-the-week boxes. The blue compartments for the morning, the red ones for the evening. I do everything right! I really do. But they don't believe it. So that's why they're watching. They want to catch me slipping up. They want to see if I forget to come home on time, or if I stop at the grocery store for too long, just to prove that I'm unfit to take care of him. It's not about hurting me, it's about catching me in a mistake so they can say they were right about me all along. They just think I'm not good enough, even though I work my fingers to the bone every single day for that man.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other situations where you feel people are acting against you or monitoring you, outside of the concern about caring for your husband?\n\nPatient: Well, no, it always comes back to him. It always comes back to my husband and the house, because that's my whole life, isn't it? I mean, like I said when I first sat down here, the whole reason I'm even talking to you is because people keep acting like I'm just... imagining all this. Like I'm making it up out of thin air! My sister even said to me on the phone last Tuesday—or maybe it was Wednesday, because I was dusting the Miller's living room and they always have me come on Wednesdays now—she said, \"Oh, you're just stressed, you're just seeing things.\" \n\nBut I'm not! I know what I see. I know for an absolute fact that the hospital people, the ones who discharged him last month, and even some of the supervisors from the housekeeping agency, they are watching our house. And they don't just stay at the house, either. They follow my cleaning routes. I've got five houses on the west side, like I told you, and I drive my little blue sedan from one to the next, and I see them. I see the same silver car parked down the block, or someone standing near the bus stop who doesn't ever get on a bus. They just wait, and they watch me hauling my vacuum out of the trunk, watching to see how long I take, checking to see if I'm rushing through my work so I can get home, or if I'm taking too long and leaving him alone. It's all about whether I'm taking proper care of him. They're just waiting for me to fail, waiting to point the finger and say I can't handle it. But I'm not crazy, and I'm not imagining it, no matter what anybody says. I see them with my own two eyes.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts or beliefs that others are acting against you or monitoring you in ways that don't involve your husband or your role as a caregiver?\n\nPatient: Well, doctor, like I was just trying to explain... I mean, it feels like we're going in circles a little bit, because this is exactly why I'm sitting here in this chair today. I'm here because people keep acting like I'm just imagining it all. They look at me like I'm making up stories, but I'm not! I know what I see. \n\nI don't have any other things going on, no other reasons for people to follow me. It is entirely about him. I know for a fact that the hospital people—the ones who were so judgmental when he was discharged—and some of the folks from the housekeeping agency I work for have been watching our house. And they don't just stay parked on our street, either. They have been watching my cleaning routes, following me when I drive from a deep clean over on Elm Street to a standard wipe-down on Maple. I see them keeping tabs on me while I'm lugging my mops and buckets around. \n\nAnd it is all just to see if I'm taking proper care of my husband. That's the only reason. There's no other big secret going on. They just don't believe a woman who cleans houses all day can also be a good wife to a sick man. They want to catch me slipping up, they want to see if I'm neglecting him, which I'm not! I do everything for him. But nobody listens to me when I tell them what's happening. They just act like it's all in my head, and it just wears me out having to keep explaining myself when I know exactly what they're doing.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever had beliefs that people were acting against you or watching you before your husband became ill, or before your current caregiving responsibilities began?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. If the question touches several topics, return the one it is\n  most directly about — never more than one.\n- Copy `key`, `name` and `description` exactly as they appear in [Symptom Profiles].\n- If your profile does not cover what the doctor asked, return the text \"None\" for `key`,\n  `name` and `description`, and say why in `reason`. Do NOT force an unrelated item.\n- On your first reply of the consultation, always return the item whose key is your\n  chief_complaint_symptom_code, whatever the doctor opened with.\n- Do not answer the doctor, and do not write anything outside the JSON.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"key\": \"<key>\", \"name\": \"<name>\", \"description\": \"<description>\", \"reason\": \"<why this item>\"}\n"
  },

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Before he got sick? Oh, heavens no. No, no. Before all this happened with his health, we were just... well, we were practically invisible, just the way I liked it! We'd go to the diner down the street on Friday nights for the fish fry—they do a wonderful battered cod, with the tartar sauce in those little paper cups—and nobody paid us any mind at all. I'd go to work, clean my houses, come home, and that was that. Nobody cared what time I left or what time I came back. 

It really wasn't until his health took that bad turn and I had to start doing absolutely everything for him. Let me think... if I count back on the calendar, it's been right about 11 months now. Yes, 11 months, because I remember it was right around the time Mrs. Higgins wanted me to do her big spring cleaning. She always insists I scrub her baseboards by hand, you know, on my hands and knees with a bucket of warm soapy water and a sponge, absolutely no mops allowed! And I was just so exhausted because my husband had just started needing me around the clock, and I was stretched so thin. That's exactly when all this nonsense started. 

The voices and the feeling that people are watching us have been happening for about 11 months now. It all just started right together. I’d be scrubbing those baseboards at the Higgins house and I'd hear the voices whispering about how I was neglecting him and doing a bad job, and then I’d look out her front window and see that same silver car parked by the big oak tree across the street. So, no, it definitely wasn't happening before. It’s been 11 months of this constant scrutiny, 11 months of them watching my every move and the voices chiming in, just waiting for me to make one little mistake with his care. Why would they watch me before? I was just a regular housekeeper then. Now, to them, I'm a housekeeper who they think is a terrible wife, and they just won't let up.

And not just the house, they've been following my cleaning routes, too. I clean a lot of houses over on the west side, you know, the big ones with the long driveways, and I've noticed the same cars parked down the street, or people just standing around when I'm taking the trash out. But I make his meals before I leave, I make sure his pills are laid out... and still, they're watching. I can feel them watching, trying to catch me making a mistake. It's just exhausting, you know? Always having to look over my shoulder while I'm just trying to do my job and take care of my family.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe people are watching or following you because they want to harm you, or do you feel like you have a special role or importance that others are trying to interfere with?\n\nPatient: Harm me? Oh, heavens no, no, I don't think they want to hurt me physically or anything like that. And I'm certainly nobody special, just a regular woman trying to keep her head above water, you know? I'm not a politician or a celebrity, I'm just a housekeeper. I've been cleaning houses for, gosh, going on thirty years now. Started right out of high school when my aunt got me a job at the downtown hotel, and then I moved to residential because the hours were better for my family. So I'm just a normal person. It's not about me being important or having some big secret role. \n\nIt's really just about my husband, and how they think I'm failing him. You see, he has had these terrible problems with his health, and he just sits in his recliner all day—that big brown leather one we got on sale a few years ago, it has a little tear in the arm now but he won't let me put a patch on it—and he really can't do much for himself right now. And people talk. Oh, you know how people talk, especially the nurses and the social workers from when he was in the hospital last time. They gave me these looks, these awful, judgmental looks when they were going over his discharge papers, like they thought I wasn't paying attention. \n\nThey see me leaving at 6:00 AM with my supplies, and they think, \"Look at her, leaving that poor sick man all alone all day while she goes off to work.\" But they don't see what goes on inside our home! They don't see that I've already made his oatmeal, and I've put his pills in those little plastic days-of-the-week boxes. The blue compartments for the morning, the red ones for the evening. I do everything right! I really do. But they don't believe it. So that's why they're watching. They want to catch me slipping up. They want to see if I forget to come home on time, or if I stop at the grocery store for too long, just to prove that I'm unfit to take care of him. It's not about hurting me, it's about catching me in a mistake so they can say they were right about me all along. They just think I'm not good enough, even though I work my fingers to the bone every single day for that man.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other situations where you feel people are acting against you or monitoring you, outside of the concern about caring for your husband?\n\nPatient: Well, no, it always comes back to him. It always comes back to my husband and the house, because that's my whole life, isn't it? I mean, like I said when I first sat down here, the whole reason I'm even talking to you is because people keep acting like I'm just... imagining all this. Like I'm making it up out of thin air! My sister even said to me on the phone last Tuesday—or maybe it was Wednesday, because I was dusting the Miller's living room and they always have me come on Wednesdays now—she said, \"Oh, you're just stressed, you're just seeing things.\" \n\nBut I'm not! I know what I see. I know for an absolute fact that the hospital people, the ones who discharged him last month, and even some of the supervisors from the housekeeping agency, they are watching our house. I've got five houses on the west side, like I told you, and I drive my little blue sedan from one to the next, and I see them. I see the same silver car parked down the block, or someone standing near the bus stop who doesn't ever get on a bus. They just wait, and they watch me hauling my vacuum out of the trunk, watching to see how long I take, checking to see if I'm rushing through my work so I can get home, or if I'm taking too long and leaving him alone. It's all about whether I'm taking proper care of him. They're just waiting for me to fail, waiting to point the finger and say I can't handle it. But I'm not crazy, and I'm not imagining it, no matter what anybody says. I see them with my own two eyes.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts or beliefs that others are acting against you or monitoring you in ways that don't involve your husband or your role as a caregiver?\n\nPatient: Well, doctor, like I was just trying to explain... I mean, it feels like we're going in circles a little bit, because this is exactly why I'm sitting here in this chair today. I'm here because people keep acting like I'm just imagining it all. They look at me like I'm making up stories, but I'm not! I know what I see. \n\nI don't have any other things going on, no other reasons for people to follow me. It is entirely about him. I know for a fact that the hospital people—the ones who were so judgmental when he was discharged—and some of the folks from the housekeeping agency I work for have been watching our house. And they don't just stay parked on our street, either. They have been watching my cleaning routes, following me when I drive from a deep clean over on Elm Street to a standard wipe-down on Maple. I see them keeping tabs on me while I'm lugging my mops and buckets around. \n\nAnd it is all just to see if I'm taking proper care of my husband. That's the only reason. They just act like it's all in my head, and it just wears me out having to keep explaining myself when I know exactly what they're doing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs that people were acting against you or watching you before your husband became ill, or before your current caregiving responsibilities began?\n\nPatient: Before he got sick? Oh, heavens no. No, no. Before all this happened with his health, we were just... well, we were practically invisible, just the way I liked it! We'd go to the diner down the street on Friday nights for the fish fry—they do a wonderful battered cod, with the tartar sauce in those little paper cups—and nobody paid us any mind at all. I'd go to work, clean my houses, come home, and that was that. Nobody cared what time I left or what time I came back. \n\nIt really wasn't until his health took that bad turn and I had to start doing absolutely everything for him. Let me think... if I count back on the calendar, it's been right about 11 months now. Yes, 11 months, because I remember it was right around the time Mrs. Higgins wanted me to do her big spring cleaning. She always insists I scrub her baseboards by hand, you know, on my hands and knees with a bucket of warm soapy water and a sponge, absolutely no mops allowed! And I was just so exhausted because my husband had just started needing me around the clock, and I was stretched so thin. That's exactly when all this nonsense started. \n\nThe voices and the feeling that people are watching us have been happening for about 11 months now. It all just started right together. I’d be scrubbing those baseboards at the Higgins house and I'd hear the voices whispering about how I was neglecting him and doing a bad job, and then I’d look out her front window and see that same silver car parked by the big oak tree across the street. So, no, it definitely wasn't happening before. It’s been 11 months of this constant scrutiny, 11 months of them watching my every move and the voices chiming in, just waiting for me to make one little mistake with his care. Why would they watch me before? I was just a regular housekeeper then. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Doctor, are you... are you actually writing down what I'm saying to you? Because I feel like I'm a broken record here, and we just keep going over the exact same ground. No. No, I have never had people acting against me or watching me when it wasn't about him. 

Because, honestly, what else is there? There is no "outside of caring for him" for me anymore. My whole world is just cleaning other people's dirt and making sure my husband is taken care of. Even if I'm just running to the grocery store—like on Sunday afternoons when I go to get his low-sodium chicken broth, because his regular doctor was very strict about his salt intake and now I have to stand there reading the back of every single can in aisle four just to make sure I don't get the wrong one—even then, if I see someone from the hospital lingering near the canned vegetables, it is entirely because they want to see if I'm buying him the right food. It always comes back to my role as his caregiver. 

I don't have some exciting secret life. I'm not anybody important enough to be followed for any other reason! I'm a 57-year-old woman who smells like bleach and lemon Pledge half the time. Nobody cares about me just for me. They only care because they think I'm failing him and they want to catch me doing it. I really don't know how many different ways I can explain this to you. It's like you're trying to dig up some other reason, but there isn't one. It is just about him, and it has only ever been about him. Do you see what I mean now, or are you going to ask me the same thing again?

Always having to look over my shoulder while I'm just trying to do my job and take care of my family.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe people are watching or following you because they want to harm you, or do you feel like you have a special role or importance that others are trying to interfere with?\n\nPatient: Harm me? I'm not a politician or a celebrity, I'm just a housekeeper. I've been cleaning houses for, gosh, going on thirty years now. Started right out of high school when my aunt got me a job at the downtown hotel, and then I moved to residential because the hours were better for my family. So I'm just a normal person. It's not about me being important or having some big secret role. \n\nIt's really just about my husband, and how they think I'm failing him. You see, he has had these terrible problems with his health, and he just sits in his recliner all day—that big brown leather one we got on sale a few years ago, it has a little tear in the arm now but he won't let me put a patch on it—and he really can't do much for himself right now. And people talk. Oh, you know how people talk, especially the nurses and the social workers from when he was in the hospital last time. They gave me these looks, these awful, judgmental looks when they were going over his discharge papers, like they thought I wasn't paying attention. \n\nThey see me leaving at 6:00 AM with my supplies, and they think, \"Look at her, leaving that poor sick man all alone all day while she goes off to work.\" But they don't see what goes on inside our home! They don't see that I've already made his oatmeal, and I've put his pills in those little plastic days-of-the-week boxes. The blue compartments for the morning, the red ones for the evening. I do everything right! I really do. But they don't believe it. So that's why they're watching. They want to catch me slipping up. They want to see if I forget to come home on time, or if I stop at the grocery store for too long, just to prove that I'm unfit to take care of him. It's not about hurting me, it's about catching me in a mistake so they can say they were right about me all along. They just think I'm not good enough, even though I work my fingers to the bone every single day for that man.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other situations where you feel people are acting against you or monitoring you, outside of the concern about caring for your husband?\n\nPatient: Well, no, it always comes back to him. It always comes back to my husband and the house, because that's my whole life, isn't it? I mean, like I said when I first sat down here, the whole reason I'm even talking to you is because people keep acting like I'm just... imagining all this. Like I'm making it up out of thin air! My sister even said to me on the phone last Tuesday—or maybe it was Wednesday, because I was dusting the Miller's living room and they always have me come on Wednesdays now—she said, \"Oh, you're just stressed, you're just seeing things.\" \n\nBut I'm not! I know what I see. I know for an absolute fact that the hospital people, the ones who discharged him last month, and even some of the supervisors from the housekeeping agency, they are watching our house. And they don't just stay at the house, either. They follow my cleaning routes. I've got five houses on the west side, like I told you, and I drive my little blue sedan from one to the next, and I see them. I see the same silver car parked down the block, or someone standing near the bus stop who doesn't ever get on a bus. They just wait, and they watch me hauling my vacuum out of the trunk, watching to see how long I take, checking to see if I'm rushing through my work so I can get home, or if I'm taking too long and leaving him alone. It's all about whether I'm taking proper care of him. They're just waiting for me to fail, waiting to point the finger and say I can't handle it. But I'm not crazy, and I'm not imagining it, no matter what anybody says. I see them with my own two eyes.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts or beliefs that others are acting against you or monitoring you in ways that don't involve your husband or your role as a caregiver?\n\nPatient: Well, doctor, like I was just trying to explain... I mean, it feels like we're going in circles a little bit, because this is exactly why I'm sitting here in this chair today. I'm here because people keep acting like I'm just imagining it all. They look at me like I'm making up stories, but I'm not! I know what I see. \n\nI don't have any other things going on, no other reasons for people to follow me. It is entirely about him. I know for a fact that the hospital people—the ones who were so judgmental when he was discharged—and some of the folks from the housekeeping agency I work for have been watching our house. And they don't just stay parked on our street, either. They have been watching my cleaning routes, following me when I drive from a deep clean over on Elm Street to a standard wipe-down on Maple. I see them keeping tabs on me while I'm lugging my mops and buckets around. \n\nAnd it is all just to see if I'm taking proper care of my husband. That's the only reason. There's no other big secret going on. They just don't believe a woman who cleans houses all day can also be a good wife to a sick man. They want to catch me slipping up, they want to see if I'm neglecting him, which I'm not! I do everything for him. But nobody listens to me when I tell them what's happening. They just act like it's all in my head, and it just wears me out having to keep explaining myself when I know exactly what they're doing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs that people were acting against you or watching you before your husband became ill, or before your current caregiving responsibilities began?\n\nPatient: Before he got sick? Oh, heavens no. No, no. Before all this happened with his health, we were just... well, we were practically invisible, just the way I liked it! I'd go to work, clean my houses, come home, and that was that. That's exactly when all this nonsense started. \n\nThe voices and the feeling that people are watching us have been happening for about 11 months now. It all just started right together. I’d be scrubbing those baseboards at the Higgins house and I'd hear the voices whispering about how I was neglecting him and doing a bad job, and then I’d look out her front window and see that same silver car parked by the big oak tree across the street. people keep acting like I'm imagining it, but I know the hospital people and some folks from the agency have been watching our house and my cleaning routes to see if I'm taking proper care of my husband.\n- key: S020\n  name: Hallucinations\n  description: Perception-like experiences that occur without an external stimulus and are as vivid and clear as normal perception.\n  manifestation: Even when the house is quiet, I hear a woman's voice near the kitchen window telling me to check the locks or move his pills, clear as if she is standing right there.\n- key: S021\n  name: Disorganized_Thinking_Speech\n  description: Symptoms where speech or thought appears scattered and difficult to follow due to a lack of logical or consistent flow.\n  manifestation: When I try to explain what is happening, the words run from my husband's heart pills to the church schedule to the numbers on my client list, and people say they can't follow me.\n- key: S022\n  name: Disorganized_Abnormal_Behavior\n  description: A state where behavior and motor control are severely disturbed, appearing as movements or reactions inappropriate to the situation.\n  manifestation: At work or at home I catch myself lining up bottles, towels, and chairs in certain patterns and walking back through the rooms over and over because it feels wrong to leave them any other way.\n- key: S033\n  name: Elevated_Irritable_Mood\n  description: A distinct period of abnormally and persistently elevated, expansive, or irritable mood.\n  manifestation: There are stretches when I feel bright and strong like I can handle everything, but if my husband or a client questions me I snap much harder than I mean to.\n- key: S034\n  name: Increased_Activity\n  description: Abnormally and even when everyone tells me to slow down.\n- key: S035\n  name: Inflated_Self_Esteem\n  description: Inflated self-esteem or grandiosity (may range from uncritical self-confidence to delusional proportions).\n  manifestation: I started feeling like I was the only one with the sense and strength to keep my husband, my home, and even some of the church people safe from what was coming.\n- key: S036\n  name: Decreased_Sleep\n  description: Decreased need for sleep (e.g., feels rested after only 3 hours of sleep).\n  manifestation: Many nights I sleep two or three hours and wake up ready to scrub floors or sort papers, not tired like I used to be after a short night.\n- key: S038\n  name: Racing_Thoughts\n  description: Flight of ideas or subjective experience that thoughts are racing.\n  manifestation: My mind jumps so fast from bills to Bible verses to my husband's breathing to which house I need to clean next that I can't catch one thought long enough to finish it.\n- key: S039\n  name: Manic_Distractibility\n  description: Attention is too easily drawn to unimportant or irrelevant external stimuli.\n  manifestation: If a car door shuts outside, the refrigerator hums, or someone moves a cup while I'm talking, my attention goes straight to that and I lose what I was doing.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: psychotic_symptoms and manic_episode occur concurrently\n  manifestation: During the weeks when I was sleeping very little and running from cleaning jobs to church errands, I was also hearing that woman's voice and feeling certain people were monitoring my routes.\n- key: additional_requirement_2\n  name: Additional Requirement 2\n  description: manic_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness\n  manifestation: For most of this whole change, I have been keyed up, irritable, and driven to keep moving, even in the stretches when things were not at their worst.\n- key: additional_requirement_3\n  name: Additional Requirement 3\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode\n  manifestation: There was a quieter spell for several weeks when I was sleeping more and not rushing around as much, but I still heard the voice by the kitchen window and believed the agency was watching us.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: My cleaning agency took away my supervisory routes because clients and coworkers said they could not rely on my work the way they used to. At home, my husband and pastor have had to step in more because I am not keeping up with caregiving and household responsibilities safely.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 year and 2 months\n  manifestation: This whole change has been going on for about 1 year and 2 months, since after my husband's cardiac hospitalization.\n- key: psychotic_symptoms_duration\n  name: Psychotic Symptoms Duration\n  description: 11 months\n  manifestation: The voices and the feeling that people are watching us have been happening for about 11 months now.\n- key: manic_episode_duration\n  name: Manic Episode Duration\n  description: 10 months\n  manifestation: The stretches of being unusually energized, irritable, and barely sleeping have been happening on and off for about 10 months.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 57 / maid_or_housekeeping_cleaner / high_school\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S019\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts or beliefs that others are acting against you or monitoring you in ways that don't involve your husband or your role as a caregiver?\n\nPatient: Well, doctor, like I was just trying to explain... I mean, it feels like we're going in circles a little bit, because this is exactly why I'm sitting here in this chair today. I'm here because people keep acting like I'm just imagining it all. They look at me like I'm making up stories, but I'm not! I know what I see. \n\nI don't have any other things going on, no other reasons for people to follow me. It is entirely about him. I know for a fact that the hospital people—the ones who were so judgmental when he was discharged—and some of the folks from the housekeeping agency I work for have been watching our house. And they don't just stay parked on our street, either. They have been watching my cleaning routes, following me when I drive from a deep clean over on Elm Street to a standard wipe-down on Maple. I see them keeping tabs on me while I'm lugging my mops and buckets around. \n\nAnd it is all just to see if I'm taking proper care of my husband. That's the only reason. There's no other big secret going on. They just don't believe a woman who cleans houses all day can also be a good wife to a sick man. They want to catch me slipping up, they want to see if I'm neglecting him, which I'm not! I do everything for him. But nobody listens to me when I tell them what's happening. They just act like it's all in my head, and it just wears me out having to keep explaining myself when I know exactly what they're doing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs that people were acting against you or watching you before your husband became ill, or before your current caregiving responsibilities began?\n\nPatient: Before he got sick? Oh, heavens no. No, no. Before all this happened with his health, we were just... well, we were practically invisible, just the way I liked it! We'd go to the diner down the street on Friday nights for the fish fry—they do a wonderful battered cod, with the tartar sauce in those little paper cups—and nobody paid us any mind at all. I'd go to work, clean my houses, come home, and that was that. Nobody cared what time I left or what time I came back. \n\nIt really wasn't until his health took that bad turn and I had to start doing absolutely everything for him. Let me think... if I count back on the calendar, it's been right about 11 months now. Yes, 11 months, because I remember it was right around the time Mrs. Higgins wanted me to do her big spring cleaning. She always insists I scrub her baseboards by hand, you know, on my hands and knees with a bucket of warm soapy water and a sponge, absolutely no mops allowed! And I was just so exhausted because my husband had just started needing me around the clock, and I was stretched so thin. That's exactly when all this nonsense started. \n\nThe voices and the feeling that people are watching us have been happening for about 11 months now. It all just started right together. I’d be scrubbing those baseboards at the Higgins house and I'd hear the voices whispering about how I was neglecting him and doing a bad job, and then I’d look out her front window and see that same silver car parked by the big oak tree across the street. So, no, it definitely wasn't happening before. It’s been 11 months of this constant scrutiny, 11 months of them watching my every move and the voices chiming in, just waiting for me to make one little mistake with his care. Why would they watch me before? I was just a regular housekeeper then. Now, to them, I'm a housekeeper who they think is a terrible wife, and they just won't let up.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had experiences where you believed people were acting against you, talking about you, or watching you, when you weren’t in the role of caring for your husband?\n\nPatient: Doctor, are you... are you actually writing down what I'm saying to you? Because I feel like I'm a broken record here, and we just keep going over the exact same ground. No. No, I have never had people acting against me or watching me when it wasn't about him. \n\nBecause, honestly, what else is there? There is no \"outside of caring for him\" for me anymore. My whole world is just cleaning other people's dirt and making sure my husband is taken care of. Even if I'm just running to the grocery store—like on Sunday afternoons when I go to get his low-sodium chicken broth, because his regular doctor was very strict about his salt intake and now I have to stand there reading the back of every single can in aisle four just to make sure I don't get the wrong one—even then, if I see someone from the hospital lingering near the canned vegetables, it is entirely because they want to see if I'm buying him the right food. It always comes back to my role as his caregiver. \n\nI don't have some exciting secret life. I'm not anybody important enough to be followed for any other reason! I'm a 57-year-old woman who smells like bleach and lemon Pledge half the time. Nobody cares about me just for me. They only care because they think I'm failing him and they want to catch me doing it. I really don't know how many different ways I can explain this to you. It's like you're trying to dig up some other reason, but there isn't one. It is just about him, and it has only ever been about him. Do you see what I mean now, or are you going to ask me the same thing again?\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever had beliefs that people were watching or acting against you in any part of your life unrelated to caregiving or your husband's illness, such as at work before he became ill, in social situations, or during childhood or earlier adulthood?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. Doctor... I... I just don't understand why we are still talking about this. I really don't. It's like you're not hearing a word I'm saying. And they watch my cleaning routes, too! 

I've told you this. I've explained this. I'm not talking about my childhood, or before we got married, or some party we went to ten years ago. I'm talking about right now. I'm talking about them following me from house to house, watching me while I'm trying to earn a living, just to see if I'm taking proper care of my husband! That is the only reason. There's no other part of my life they care about. Why would they? I'm just a housekeeper. But they think I'm failing him, and they want to catch me doing it. I know they're watching me for that, and only that. So please, doctor, can we please talk about something else? Nobody cared what time I left or what time I came back. \n\nIt really wasn't until his health took that bad turn and I had to start doing absolutely everything for him. Let me think... if I count back on the calendar, it's been right about 11 months now. Yes, 11 months, because I remember it was right around the time Mrs. Higgins wanted me to do her big spring cleaning. She always insists I scrub her baseboards by hand, you know, on my hands and knees with a bucket of warm soapy water and a sponge, absolutely no mops allowed! And I was just so exhausted because my husband had just started needing me around the clock, and I was stretched so thin. That's exactly when all this nonsense started. \n\nThe voices and the feeling that people are watching us have been happening for about 11 months now. It all just started right together. I’d be scrubbing those baseboards at the Higgins house and I'd hear the voices whispering about how I was neglecting him and doing a bad job, and then I’d look out her front window and see that same silver car parked by the big oak tree across the street. So, no, it definitely wasn't happening before. It’s been 11 months of this constant scrutiny, 11 months of them watching my every move and the voices chiming in, just waiting for me to make one little mistake with his care. Why would they watch me before? I was just a regular housekeeper then. Now, to them, I'm a housekeeper who they think is a terrible wife, and they just won't let up.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had experiences where you believed people were acting against you, talking about you, or watching you, when you weren’t in the role of caring for your husband?\n\nPatient: Doctor, are you... are you actually writing down what I'm saying to you? Because I feel like I'm a broken record here, and we just keep going over the exact same ground. No. No, I have never had people acting against me or watching me when it wasn't about him. \n\nBecause, honestly, what else is there? There is no \"outside of caring for him\" for me anymore. My whole world is just cleaning other people's dirt and making sure my husband is taken care of. Even if I'm just running to the grocery store—like on Sunday afternoons when I go to get his low-sodium chicken broth, because his regular doctor was very strict about his salt intake and now I have to stand there reading the back of every single can in aisle four just to make sure I don't get the wrong one—even then, if I see someone from the hospital lingering near the canned vegetables, it is entirely because they want to see if I'm buying him the right food. It always comes back to my role as his caregiver. \n\nI don't have some exciting secret life. I'm not anybody important enough to be followed for any other reason! I'm a 57-year-old woman who smells like bleach and lemon Pledge half the time. Nobody cares about me just for me. They only care because they think I'm failing him and they want to catch me doing it. I really don't know how many different ways I can explain this to you. It's like you're trying to dig up some other reason, but there isn't one. It is just about him, and it has only ever been about him. Do you see what I mean now, or are you going to ask me the same thing again?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs that people were watching or acting against you in any part of your life unrelated to caregiving or your husband's illness, such as at work before he became ill, in social situations, or during childhood or earlier adulthood?\n\nPatient: Doctor... I... I just don't understand why we are still talking about this. I really don't. It's like you're not hearing a word I'm saying. I'm sitting right here, telling you exactly what is happening to me, and you just keep asking the same question over and over like you expect a different answer! \n\nThis is exactly why I came in today. Because everyone acts like I'm just making this up, like I'm crazy or imagining things. But I'm not! I know what I see with my own two eyes. I know that the hospital people—the ones who were so snotty and judgmental when my husband was discharged, the ones with the little clipboards who kept giving me those looks—and some of the folks from the housekeeping agency I work for, they have been watching our house. And they watch my cleaning routes, too! \n\nI've told you this. I've explained this. I'm not talking about my childhood, or before we got married, or some party we went to ten years ago. I'm talking about right now. I'm talking about them following me from house to house, watching me while I'm trying to earn a living, just to see if I'm taking proper care of my husband! That is the only reason. There's no other part of my life they care about. Why would they? I'm just a housekeeper. But they think I'm failing him, and they want to catch me doing it. I know they're watching me for that, and only that. So please, doctor, can we please talk about something else? Because I feel like I'm losing my mind having to say the same thing five times in a row.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever had experiences where you believed people were plotting against you, talking about you behind your back, or watching you, even during times when you were not responsible for caring for your husband?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 57 / maid_or_housekeeping_cleaner / high_school\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"I'm here because people keep acting like I'm imagining it, but I know the hospital people and some folks from the agency have been watching our house and my cleaning routes to see if I'm taking proper care of my husband.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Are you... is this some kind of memory test they make you do? Because I swear to you, Doctor, you just asked me the exact same thing not two minutes ago. I feel like I'm talking to a brick wall! I am sitting here, pouring my heart out, telling you that the whole reason I'm even here in this clinic today is because people keep acting like I'm imagining it. Everyone treats me like I'm just making up stories because I'm stressed out or tired from the cleaning. 

But I am not blind! I know what's happening. I know the hospital people—especially that one discharge nurse with the tight bun who practically shoved his care manual at me like I was an idiot—and some folks from the cleaning agency, they have absolutely been watching our house. They sit out there, and then they follow my cleaning routes! I'll be driving my little Toyota over to the west side of town to do a deep clean for the Smiths, dragging my vacuum and all my supplies out of the trunk, and there they are, just keeping tabs on me. 

And it is entirely, one hundred percent, just to see if I'm taking proper care of my husband! There is no other time. There is no other reason. They aren't plotting against me because of who I am, they aren't talking behind my back about my floor-waxing technique, they are doing it because they think I'm a bad caregiver and they want to catch me slipping up! I just... I don't know how else to say it to you. Do you have a different question on that clipboard, or are we going to be stuck on this one all afternoon? Because I really need you to hear what I'm actually saying instead of digging for some other answer that just doesn't exist.

Always having to look over my shoulder while I'm just trying to do my job and take care of my family.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe people are watching or following you because they want to harm you, or do you feel like you have a special role or importance that others are trying to interfere with?\n\nPatient: Harm me? You see, he has had these terrible problems with his health, and he just sits in his recliner all day—that big brown leather one we got on sale a few years ago, it has a little tear in the arm now but he won't let me put a patch on it—and he really can't do much for himself right now. They gave me these looks, these awful, judgmental looks when they were going over his discharge papers, like they thought I wasn't paying attention. \n\nThey see me leaving at 6:00 AM with my supplies, and they think, \"Look at her, leaving that poor sick man all alone all day while she goes off to work.\" But they don't see what goes on inside our home! They don't see that I've already made his oatmeal, and I've put his pills in those little plastic days-of-the-week boxes. The blue compartments for the morning, the red ones for the evening. I do everything right! I really do. But they don't believe it. So that's why they're watching. They want to catch me slipping up. It always comes back to my husband and the house, because that's my whole life, isn't it? My sister even said to me on the phone last Tuesday—or maybe it was Wednesday, because I was dusting the Miller's living room and they always have me come on Wednesdays now—she said, \"Oh, you're just stressed, you're just seeing things.\" \n\nBut I'm not! I know what I see. I know for an absolute fact that the hospital people, the ones who discharged him last month, and even some of the supervisors from the housekeeping agency, they are watching our house. But I'm not crazy, and I'm not imagining it, no matter what anybody says. I mean, it feels like we're going in circles a little bit, because this is exactly why I'm sitting here in this chair today. I'm here because people keep acting like I'm just imagining it all. They look at me like I'm making up stories, but I'm not! I know what I see. \n\nI don't have any other things going on, no other reasons for people to follow me. It is entirely about him. I know for a fact that the hospital people—the ones who were so judgmental when he was discharged—and some of the folks from the housekeeping agency I work for have been watching our house. I see them keeping tabs on me while I'm lugging my mops and buckets around. \n\nAnd it is all just to see if I'm taking proper care of my husband. That's the only reason. There's no other big secret going on. They just don't believe a woman who cleans houses all day can also be a good wife to a sick man. They want to catch me slipping up, they want to see if I'm neglecting him, which I'm not! I do everything for him. But nobody listens to me when I tell them what's happening. They just act like it's all in my head, and it just wears me out having to keep explaining myself when I know exactly what they're doing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs that people were acting against you or watching you before your husband became ill, or before your current caregiving responsibilities began?\n\nPatient: Before he got sick? Oh, heavens no. No, no. Before all this happened with his health, we were just... well, we were practically invisible, just the way I liked it! We'd go to the diner down the street on Friday nights for the fish fry—they do a wonderful battered cod, with the tartar sauce in those little paper cups—and nobody paid us any mind at all. I'd go to work, clean my houses, come home, and that was that. Nobody cared what time I left or what time I came back. \n\nIt really wasn't until his health took that bad turn and I had to start doing absolutely everything for him. Let me think... if I count back on the calendar, it's been right about 11 months now. Yes, 11 months, because I remember it was right around the time Mrs. Higgins wanted me to do her big spring cleaning. She always insists I scrub her baseboards by hand, you know, on my hands and knees with a bucket of warm soapy water and a sponge, absolutely no mops allowed! And I was just so exhausted because my husband had just started needing me around the clock, and I was stretched so thin. That's exactly when all this nonsense started. \n\nThe voices and the feeling that people are watching us have been happening for about 11 months now. It all just started right together. I’d be scrubbing those baseboards at the Higgins house and I'd hear the voices whispering about how I was neglecting him and doing a bad job, and then I’d look out her front window and see that same silver car parked by the big oak tree across the street. So, no, it definitely wasn't happening before. It’s been 11 months of this constant scrutiny, 11 months of them watching my every move and the voices chiming in, just waiting for me to make one little mistake with his care. Why would they watch me before? I was just a regular housekeeper then. Now, to them, I'm a housekeeper who they think is a terrible wife, and they just won't let up.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had experiences where you believed people were acting against you, talking about you, or watching you, when you weren’t in the role of caring for your husband?\n\nPatient: Doctor, are you... are you actually writing down what I'm saying to you? Because I feel like I'm a broken record here, and we just keep going over the exact same ground. No. No, I have never had people acting against me or watching me when it wasn't about him. \n\nBecause, honestly, what else is there? There is no \"outside of caring for him\" for me anymore. My whole world is just cleaning other people's dirt and making sure my husband is taken care of. Even if I'm just running to the grocery store—like on Sunday afternoons when I go to get his low-sodium chicken broth, because his regular doctor was very strict about his salt intake and now I have to stand there reading the back of every single can in aisle four just to make sure I don't get the wrong one—even then, if I see someone from the hospital lingering near the canned vegetables, it is entirely because they want to see if I'm buying him the right food. It always comes back to my role as his caregiver. \n\nI don't have some exciting secret life. I'm not anybody important enough to be followed for any other reason! I'm a 57-year-old woman who smells like bleach and lemon Pledge half the time. Nobody cares about me just for me. They only care because they think I'm failing him and they want to catch me doing it. I really don't know how many different ways I can explain this to you. It's like you're trying to dig up some other reason, but there isn't one. It is just about him, and it has only ever been about him. Do you see what I mean now, or are you going to ask me the same thing again?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs that people were watching or acting against you in any part of your life unrelated to caregiving or your husband's illness, such as at work before he became ill, in social situations, or during childhood or earlier adulthood?\n\nPatient: Doctor... I... I just don't understand why we are still talking about this. I really don't. It's like you're not hearing a word I'm saying. I'm sitting right here, telling you exactly what is happening to me, and you just keep asking the same question over and over like you expect a different answer! \n\nThis is exactly why I came in today. Because everyone acts like I'm just making this up, like I'm crazy or imagining things. But I'm not! I know what I see with my own two eyes. I know that the hospital people—the ones who were so snotty and judgmental when my husband was discharged, the ones with the little clipboards who kept giving me those looks—and some of the folks from the housekeeping agency I work for, they have been watching our house. And they watch my cleaning routes, too! \n\nI've told you this. I've explained this. I'm not talking about my childhood, or before we got married, or some party we went to ten years ago. I'm talking about right now. I'm talking about them following me from house to house, watching me while I'm trying to earn a living, just to see if I'm taking proper care of my husband! That is the only reason. There's no other part of my life they care about. Why would they? I'm just a housekeeper. But they think I'm failing him, and they want to catch me doing it. I know they're watching me for that, and only that. So please, doctor, can we please talk about something else? Because I feel like I'm losing my mind having to say the same thing five times in a row.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had experiences where you believed people were plotting against you, talking about you behind your back, or watching you, even during times when you were not responsible for caring for your husband?\n\nPatient: Are you... is this some kind of memory test they make you do? Because I swear to you, Doctor, you just asked me the exact same thing not two minutes ago. I feel like I'm talking to a brick wall! I am sitting here, pouring my heart out, telling you that the whole reason I'm even here in this clinic today is because people keep acting like I'm imagining it. Everyone treats me like I'm just making up stories because I'm stressed out or tired from the cleaning. \n\nBut I am not blind! I know what's happening. I know the hospital people—especially that one discharge nurse with the tight bun who practically shoved his care manual at me like I was an idiot—and some folks from the cleaning agency, they have absolutely been watching our house. They sit out there, and then they follow my cleaning routes! I'll be driving my little Toyota over to the west side of town to do a deep clean for the Smiths, dragging my vacuum and all my supplies out of the trunk, and there they are, just keeping tabs on me. \n\nAnd it is entirely, one hundred percent, just to see if I'm taking proper care of my husband! There is no other time. There is no other reason. They aren't plotting against me because of who I am, they aren't talking behind my back about my floor-waxing technique, they are doing it because they think I'm a bad caregiver and they want to catch me slipping up! I just... I don't know how else to say it to you. Do you have a different question on that clipboard, or are we going to be stuck on this one all afternoon? Because I really need you to hear what I'm actually saying instead of digging for some other answer that just doesn't exist.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Are you... I mean, are you actually listening to me? Because I feel like I'm trapped in some sort of loop here. Honestly, Doctor, I don't know if you're trying to trick me or if you just have a checklist you're forced to read from, but I've answered this. I've told you. I've told you four times now! 

I am sitting in this chair, in this office, taking time out of my day when I could be at home making sure my husband's oxygen tank is properly calibrated—which is a whole ordeal in itself, let me tell you, because the dial sticks and if you don't tap it just right, it looks like it's on three liters but it's really on two—I'm here because people keep acting like I'm imagining things. They look at me like I'm the one who's crazy. My sister does it, the neighbors do it, everyone does it. But I know what I know. 

I know the hospital people—and I mean the administration, the nurses, the social workers, all of them—and some folks from the housekeeping agency I work for, they have been watching our house. They park down the street. They watch my cleaning routes. Just last Tuesday, I was at the Johnsons' place out in the suburbs, scrubbing their baseboards, which is back-breaking work at my age, and I looked out their front window and there was a dark sedan parked by the fire hydrant. Just sitting there! And it's all to see if I'm taking proper care of my husband. That's it. That's the only reason. 

There is no "before he became ill." There is no other part of my life. My life is him, and taking care of him, and them watching me to make sure I don't mess it up. I don't know how to make this any clearer to you. I really don't. Can we please, please talk about something else? Because I'm getting a headache having to repeat myself over and over.

I mean, it feels like we're going in circles a little bit, because this is exactly why I'm sitting here in this chair today. I'm here because people keep acting like I'm just imagining it all. They look at me like I'm making up stories, but I'm not! I know what I see. \n\nI don't have any other things going on, no other reasons for people to follow me. It is entirely about him. I know for a fact that the hospital people—the ones who were so judgmental when he was discharged—and some of the folks from the housekeeping agency I work for have been watching our house. And they don't just stay parked on our street, either. They have been watching my cleaning routes, following me when I drive from a deep clean over on Elm Street to a standard wipe-down on Maple. They want to catch me slipping up, they want to see if I'm neglecting him, which I'm not! I do everything for him. But nobody listens to me when I tell them what's happening. They just act like it's all in my head, and it just wears me out having to keep explaining myself when I know exactly what they're doing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs that people were acting against you or watching you before your husband became ill, or before your current caregiving responsibilities began?\n\nPatient: Before he got sick? Oh, heavens no. No, no. Before all this happened with his health, we were just... well, we were practically invisible, just the way I liked it! We'd go to the diner down the street on Friday nights for the fish fry—they do a wonderful battered cod, with the tartar sauce in those little paper cups—and nobody paid us any mind at all. I'd go to work, clean my houses, come home, and that was that. Nobody cared what time I left or what time I came back. \n\nIt really wasn't until his health took that bad turn and I had to start doing absolutely everything for him. Yes, 11 months, because I remember it was right around the time Mrs. Higgins wanted me to do her big spring cleaning. She always insists I scrub her baseboards by hand, you know, on my hands and knees with a bucket of warm soapy water and a sponge, absolutely no mops allowed! And I was just so exhausted because my husband had just started needing me around the clock, and I was stretched so thin. That's exactly when all this nonsense started. \n\nThe voices and the feeling that people are watching us have been happening for about 11 months now. It all just started right together. I’d be scrubbing those baseboards at the Higgins house and I'd hear the voices whispering about how I was neglecting him and doing a bad job, and then I’d look out her front window and see that same silver car parked by the big oak tree across the street. So, no, it definitely wasn't happening before. It’s been 11 months of this constant scrutiny, 11 months of them watching my every move and the voices chiming in, just waiting for me to make one little mistake with his care. Why would they watch me before? I was just a regular housekeeper then. Now, to them, I'm a housekeeper who they think is a terrible wife, and they just won't let up.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had experiences where you believed people were acting against you, talking about you, or watching you, when you weren’t in the role of caring for your husband?\n\nPatient: Doctor, are you... are you actually writing down what I'm saying to you? No. No, I have never had people acting against me or watching me when it wasn't about him. \n\nBecause, honestly, what else is there? There is no \"outside of caring for him\" for me anymore. My whole world is just cleaning other people's dirt and making sure my husband is taken care of. Even if I'm just running to the grocery store—like on Sunday afternoons when I go to get his low-sodium chicken broth, because his regular doctor was very strict about his salt intake and now I have to stand there reading the back of every single can in aisle four just to make sure I don't get the wrong one—even then, if I see someone from the hospital lingering near the canned vegetables, it is entirely because they want to see if I'm buying him the right food. It always comes back to my role as his caregiver. \n\nI don't have some exciting secret life. I'm not anybody important enough to be followed for any other reason! I'm a 57-year-old woman who smells like bleach and lemon Pledge half the time. Nobody cares about me just for me. They only care because they think I'm failing him and they want to catch me doing it. I really don't know how many different ways I can explain this to you. It's like you're trying to dig up some other reason, but there isn't one. It is just about him, and it has only ever been about him. Do you see what I mean now, or are you going to ask me the same thing again?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs that people were watching or acting against you in any part of your life unrelated to caregiving or your husband's illness, such as at work before he became ill, in social situations, or during childhood or earlier adulthood?\n\nPatient: Doctor... I... I just don't understand why we are still talking about this. I really don't. It's like you're not hearing a word I'm saying. I'm sitting right here, telling you exactly what is happening to me, and you just keep asking the same question over and over like you expect a different answer! \n\nThis is exactly why I came in today. Because everyone acts like I'm just making this up, like I'm crazy or imagining things. But I'm not! I know what I see with my own two eyes. I know that the hospital people—the ones who were so snotty and judgmental when my husband was discharged, the ones with the little clipboards who kept giving me those looks—and some of the folks from the housekeeping agency I work for, they have been watching our house. I know they're watching me for that, and only that. So please, doctor, can we please talk about something else? Because I feel like I'm losing my mind having to say the same thing five times in a row.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had experiences where you believed people were plotting against you, talking about you behind your back, or watching you, even during times when you were not responsible for caring for your husband?\n\nPatient: Are you... is this some kind of memory test they make you do? Because I swear to you, Doctor, you just asked me the exact same thing not two minutes ago. I feel like I'm talking to a brick wall! I am sitting here, pouring my heart out, telling you that the whole reason I'm even here in this clinic today is because people keep acting like I'm imagining it. Everyone treats me like I'm just making up stories because I'm stressed out or tired from the cleaning. \n\nBut I am not blind! I know what's happening. I know the hospital people—especially that one discharge nurse with the tight bun who practically shoved his care manual at me like I was an idiot—and some folks from the cleaning agency, they have absolutely been watching our house. They sit out there, and then they follow my cleaning routes! I'll be driving my little Toyota over to the west side of town to do a deep clean for the Smiths, dragging my vacuum and all my supplies out of the trunk, and there they are, just keeping tabs on me. \n\nAnd it is entirely, one hundred percent, just to see if I'm taking proper care of my husband! There is no other time. There is no other reason. They aren't plotting against me because of who I am, they aren't talking behind my back about my floor-waxing technique, they are doing it because they think I'm a bad caregiver and they want to catch me slipping up! I just... I don't know how else to say it to you. Do you have a different question on that clipboard, or are we going to be stuck on this one all afternoon? Because I really need you to hear what I'm actually saying instead of digging for some other answer that just doesn't exist.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs that people were acting against you or watching you when you were not in the role of caring for your husband, such as at work before he became ill or in other parts of your life?\n\nPatient: Are you...
