And they don't just stay parked on our street, either. They have been watching my cleaning routes, following me when I drive from a deep clean over on Elm Street to a standard wipe-down on Maple. I see them keeping tabs on me while I'm lugging my mops and buckets around. \n\nAnd it is all just to see if I'm taking proper care of my husband. That's the only reason. There's no other big secret going on. They just don't believe a woman who cleans houses all day can also be a good wife to a sick man. They want to catch me slipping up, they want to see if I'm neglecting him, which I'm not! I do everything for him. But nobody listens to me when I tell them what's happening. They just act like it's all in my head, and it just wears me out having to keep explaining myself when I know exactly what they're doing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs that people were acting against you or watching you before your husband became ill, or before your current caregiving responsibilities began?\n\nPatient: Before he got sick? Oh, heavens no. No, no. Before all this happened with his health, we were just... well, we were practically invisible, just the way I liked it! We'd go to the diner down the street on Friday nights for the fish fry—they do a wonderful battered cod, with the tartar sauce in those little paper cups—and nobody paid us any mind at all. I'd go to work, clean my houses, come home, and that was that. Nobody cared what time I left or what time I came back. \n\nIt really wasn't until his health took that bad turn and I had to start doing absolutely everything for him. Let me think... if I count back on the calendar, it's been right about 11 months now. Yes, 11 months, because I remember it was right around the time Mrs. Higgins wanted me to do her big spring cleaning. She always insists I scrub her baseboards by hand, you know, on my hands and knees with a bucket of warm soapy water and a sponge, absolutely no mops allowed! And I was just so exhausted because my husband had just started needing me around the clock, and I was stretched so thin. That's exactly when all this nonsense started. \n\nThe voices and the feeling that people are watching us have been happening for about 11 months now. It all just started right together. I’d be scrubbing those baseboards at the Higgins house and I'd hear the voices whispering about how I was neglecting him and doing a bad job, and then I’d look out her front window and see that same silver car parked by the big oak tree across the street. So, no, it definitely wasn't happening before. It’s been 11 months of this constant scrutiny, 11 months of them watching my every move and the voices chiming in, just waiting for me to make one little mistake with his care. Why would they watch me before? I was just a regular housekeeper then. Now, to them, I'm a housekeeper who they think is a terrible wife, and they just won't let up.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had experiences where you believed people were acting against you, talking about you, or watching you, when you weren’t in the role of caring for your husband?\n\nPatient: Doctor, are you... are you actually writing down what I'm saying to you? Even if I'm just running to the grocery store—like on Sunday afternoons when I go to get his low-sodium chicken broth, because his regular doctor was very strict about his salt intake and now I have to stand there reading the back of every single can in aisle four just to make sure I don't get the wrong one—even then, if I see someone from the hospital lingering near the canned vegetables, it is entirely because they want to see if I'm buying him the right food. It's like you're stuck on a track, like a broken record. Are we on one of those hidden camera shows? Is someone behind a two-way mirror right now? Because I honestly cannot fathom why you are asking me the exact same question for the fourth time. I really can't. It's like you're trying to wear me down until I give you the answer you want, but I can't give you a lie! \n\nI am telling you, and I feel like I'm screaming it into a canyon, I am only here in this office because people keep acting like I'm imagining it. My family, the neighbors, they all treat me like I'm just making it up because I'm tired from working all day and then coming home to play nurse. But I am not crazy! I know what I see. I know for an absolute fact that the hospital people—the ones who think they know everything about home care just because they have a badge—and some of the folks from the housekeeping agency have been watching our house. They sit out there, and they follow my cleaning routes! \n\nAnd I've told you, before his lungs went bad, before he got sick, nobody cared what I did! I was just a woman going to work, scrubbing toilets, dusting ceiling fans for the Hendersons—which, by the way, requires a special extension pole that I have to lug around in my trunk, it's exhausting—and coming home. Nobody was tailing my Toyota back then! Why would they? There was nothing to check up on. It only started when he got sick, because they want to see if I'm taking proper care of my husband. They want to catch me making a mistake with his oxygen or his meds so they can say I'm an unfit caregiver. \n\nThat is it. That is the whole truth. So no. No, no, a thousand times no. Never before he got sick. Please, I am begging you, look at me, hear the words coming out of my mouth, write a big giant \"NO\" on your notepad, and let's move on. Because my husband is at home, and I need to get back to him, and we are just wasting time going in circles like this!\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any time in your life before your husband became ill when you felt people were watching you or acting against you, even slightly?\n\nPatient: Doctor... I just... I might actually start crying. Or screaming. I haven't decided yet. \"Even slightly?\" Are you serious? I feel like I'm being interrogated by a brick wall! \n\nI am telling you, for the fifth time—I am literally counting on my fingers now, look, five times—that the answer is no. I am only sitting in this room, talking to you today, because everyone around me keeps acting like I'm imagining it all. They pat my hand and tell me I'm just overworked, or stressed, or whatever word they want to use to dismiss me. But I'm not imagining it! I know what I see with my own two eyes. \n\nI know the hospital people—those care coordinators who think they know everything just because they have a clipboard—and some of the folks from the housekeeping agency I work for, I know they have been watching our house. They watch my cleaning routes! I'll be out there, scrubbing the grout at the Miller's place—and let me tell you, their master bathroom grout is a nightmare, you have to use an old toothbrush and this heavy bleach solution that completely ruins your cuticles—and I'll see them out the window. Waiting. Watching. \n\nAnd it is entirely, one hundred percent, to see if I'm taking proper care of my husband. They want to see if I'm too tired from work to manage his oxygen, if I'm going to slip up and make a mistake so they can swoop in. That's it! \n\nBefore he got sick? No! Nobody watched me before he got sick, not even slightly, because I was just an invisible middle-aged woman cleaning other people's houses! There was no reason to follow me! Please, I am begging you, I have to get home to him, can we just stop asking this exact same question?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed that people were watching or talking about you when you were not in the role of caring for your husband, even briefly or slightly, such as at work before he became ill or in other parts of your life?\n\nPatient: Doctor... I... I honestly don't know if I should walk out that door right now or if I should just scream. Are you testing me? Is this some kind of psychological test where you try to make the person feel like they're losing their mind by asking them the exact same thing six times in a row? Because it's working! \n\nI am only here, sitting in this chair, talking to you today, because people keep acting like I'm imagining it! My family, the neighbors, they all treat me like I'm just some hysterical, exhausted old woman who's seeing things because she works too hard and doesn't sleep enough. But I am not imagining it. I know what is happening. I know the hospital people—the ones who always have those little lanyards and look down their noses at you—and some folks from the agency have been watching our house and my cleaning routes! \n\nThey follow me! I'll be out at a job, like yesterday at the Thompson's—and they have this awful, porous terracotta tile in their sunroom that traps absolutely every speck of dirt, so you literally have to get down on your hands and knees with a hard bristle brush and scrub until your shoulders ache, it's just miserable, back-breaking work—and I'll look out the window, and there they are. Watching me. And it is entirely to see if I'm taking proper care of my husband. They want to see if I'm too distracted or too physically drained from scrubbing floors to do it right, so they can step in and take over. \n\nSo no, not before he got sick, not briefly, not slightly, not in any other part of my life! Before his lungs gave out, I was completely invisible. Nobody cares about a 57-year-old woman scrubbing baseboards and taking out other people's trash. They only care now because of him. Please, doctor, look at me. I have answered this over and over. Can we please, please just move on? I have to get home and make sure his oxygen is set right before lunch, and I can't keep answering the same question.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time in your life when you felt certain something was true, even though others told you it wasn’t, and it wasn’t related to caring for your husband?\n\nPatient: Doctor... I... I am literally out of breath from repeating myself. You keep changing the words around, rearranging the sentence, but it is the exact same question! \n\n\"Felt certain something was true when others said it wasn't?\" Yes! That is exactly what I have been telling you this entire time! That is the whole reason I am here! I am only sitting in this office because people keep acting like I'm imagining it! My sister, the neighbors, they all look at me with this... this horrible, patronizing pity, telling me it's not true, that I'm just making things up because I'm under stress. \n\nBut I know what I see! I know for a fact that the hospital people and some of those folks from the housekeeping agency have been watching our house and following my cleaning routes! \n\nAnd it is *never* unrelated to him! Never! Why would it be? Why on earth would anyone care about me otherwise? They don't care about me dusting the blinds at the Davis house—which, let me tell you, takes forever because they have those cheap, flimsy plastic ones that snap if you press even a little too hard, so you have to wipe every single individual slat by hand with a damp microfiber cloth, and my fingers cramp up just thinking about it. They don't care about that! They only care about whether I'm too exhausted from wiping those blinds to manage his breathing treatments when I get home! They are watching to see if I am taking proper care of my husband! \n\nThat's it! There is no other secret thing I believe. There is no other time in my life. It is just this, right now, with him! I don't know how else to explain it to you. I really, truly don't. Are we done with this topic? Because I feel like I'm going to pull my hair out just trying to get you to listen to me and move on to something else. Please, I need to get back to him. Can we just talk about something else?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something strongly that others said wasn't true, even when there was no connection to caring for your husband or your work as a housekeeper?\n\nPatient: I feel like I am actually losing my mind in this office. Are you even listening to me? You just keep tacking on new little phrases at the end of the sentence—\"no connection to your work,\" \"no connection to your husband\"—but it is the exact same question! \n\nI'm here because people keep acting like I'm imagining it! That's it! That is the only reason! My family, everyone around me, they think I've just snapped from the stress, but I know the hospital people and some folks from the agency have been watching our house and my cleaning routes. I have told you this! I know they are doing it to see if I'm taking proper care of my husband! \n\nThere is no other belief. There is no other time. Nobody is watching me for any other reason! They don't care about me outside of him. They don't care that I spend my mornings on my hands and knees polishing the brass stair rods at the Goldbergs' house—which is just a ridiculous thing to have in a modern home, by the way, because brass tarnishes if you even look at it wrong, so you have to rub this awful smelling paste into every single rod and buff it out with a rag until your knuckles are completely raw and your shoulders are screaming. Nobody cares about that! \n\nThey only care if my hands are too cramped from buffing that stupid brass to adjust his oxygen valves when I get home! They just want to catch me slipping up with his care. That is the only thing happening here! \n\nSo no! Nothing unrelated to him! Nothing unrelated to my work! Please, doctor, I am begging you, just write down \"no\" in your file and let's move on. I cannot keep answering the same question. I need to get home to him.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

I... I am actually going to start crying. I am going to sit right here in this chair and cry because I feel like you are torturing me! Are you a broken record? Is there a hidden camera in here? Why are you doing this to me?!

My sister, the neighbors, they all look at me with this... this horrible, patronizing pity, telling me it's not true, that I'm just making things up because I'm under stress. \n\nBut I know what I see! I know for a fact that the hospital people and some of those folks from the housekeeping agency have been watching our house and following my cleaning routes! \n\nAnd it is *never* unrelated to him! Never! Why would it be? Why on earth would anyone care about me otherwise? They don't care about me dusting the blinds at the Davis house—which, let me tell you, takes forever because they have those cheap, flimsy plastic ones that snap if you press even a little too hard, so you have to wipe every single individual slat by hand with a damp microfiber cloth, and my fingers cramp up just thinking about it. They don't care about that! They only care about whether I'm too exhausted from wiping those blinds to manage his breathing treatments when I get home! They are watching to see if I am taking proper care of my husband! \n\nThat's it! There is no other secret thing I believe. There is no other time in my life. It is just this, right now, with him! I don't know how else to explain it to you. I really, truly don't. Are we done with this topic? Because I feel like I'm going to pull my hair out just trying to get you to listen to me and move on to something else. Please, I need to get back to him. Can we just talk about something else?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something strongly that others said wasn't true, even when there was no connection to caring for your husband or your work as a housekeeper?\n\nPatient: I feel like I am actually losing my mind in this office. Are you even listening to me? You just keep tacking on new little phrases at the end of the sentence—\"no connection to your work,\" \"no connection to your husband\"—but it is the exact same question! \n\nI'm here because people keep acting like I'm imagining it! That's it! That is the only reason! My family, everyone around me, they think I've just snapped from the stress, but I know the hospital people and some folks from the agency have been watching our house and my cleaning routes. I have told you this! I know they are doing it to see if I'm taking proper care of my husband! \n\nThere is no other belief. There is no other time. Nobody is watching me for any other reason! They don't care about me outside of him. They don't care that I spend my mornings on my hands and knees polishing the brass stair rods at the Goldbergs' house—which is just a ridiculous thing to have in a modern home, by the way, because brass tarnishes if you even look at it wrong, so you have to rub this awful smelling paste into every single rod and buff it out with a rag until your knuckles are completely raw and your shoulders are screaming. Nobody cares about that! \n\nThey only care if my hands are too cramped from buffing that stupid brass to adjust his oxygen valves when I get home! They just want to catch me slipping up with his care. That is the only thing happening here! \n\nSo no! Nothing unrelated to him! Nothing unrelated to my work! Please, doctor, I am begging you, just write down \"no\" in your file and let's move on. I cannot keep answering the same question. I need to get home to him.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something strongly that others said wasn't true, even when it had nothing to do with caring for your husband or your job as a housekeeper?\n\nPatient: I... I am actually going to start crying. I am going to sit right here in this chair and cry because I feel like you are torturing me! Are you a broken record? Is there a hidden camera in here? Why are you doing this to me?! \n\nI said NO! N-O! Nothing! There is absolutely nothing else! \n\nDo you think I have the time or the energy to sit around coming up with wild ideas about... about the government or whatever else it is you're fishing for? I am a fifty-seven-year-old woman who cleans up other people's filth for a living! Do you know what I did this morning before I came to this appointment? I was at the Peterson house, and they have this massive, antique clawfoot tub. It's beautiful to look at, but an absolute nightmare to clean because the porcelain is so old and pitted that soap scum just bakes right into it. You have to use this harsh, toxic pumice powder that burns your throat if you breathe it in, and you have to lean your entire body weight over the edge, just crushing your ribs against the hard rim, to reach the back corners. My back was in absolute agony by the time I finished. \n\nAnd nobody on this earth cares about that! The only reason anyone from the hospital or the agency cares about me hanging over a tub breathing in chemical fumes is because they want to know if my own lungs are going to be too shot to help my husband with his breathing exercises when I get home! They want to see if I'm too exhausted to lift his oxygen tanks! \n\nThat is it! That is the only thing! I don't have any other beliefs because there *is* nothing else! I am not crazy! I am just a wife trying to keep her husband alive while people spy on her to see if she fails! Now please, I am begging you, for the love of God, ask me about his medications, ask me about my sleep, ask me about the weather—ask me *anything* else, or I am getting up and walking out that door right now!"
